### Adversarial audit (contrarian pass, each attack answered with a command)
1. Attack: "the two version bumps may be churn - already built". Checked: before the pushes the newest builds were 11089770 (umbriel, old gitdate 20261007140537) and 11089599 (logseq 2.0.1-4); the new builds carry new NVRs and both went green 4/4 (build-chroot/list output above).
2. Attack: "the first updater sweep ran from the wrong directory and may have corrupted tracked specs". Checked: after deleting the 32 untracked stray root files, `git status --porcelain` reported zero modified tracked files; the corrected re-run changed exactly two specs (git diff --stat: logseq, umbriel-git) and every other spec stayed byte-identical.
3. Attack: "teardown tokens may be recycled from an earlier execution". Checked: all 20 logs were written this execution under /tmp/td plus the two /tmp/teardown-*.log files, each carries its own BUILT= NVR, and docker ps showed the containers running at their timestamps (14:43-15:55Z today).
4. Attack: "a smoke PASS may hide a broken command behind `|| true`". Checked: no smoke used `|| true`; heroic's asserts app.asar plus a stat on chrome-sandbox; each install step installed the RPM the same container just built, and rpm -V ran for the 11 packages with a harness package name.
5. Attack: "the same-NVR rebuild wave from the guard push may still end in failures". Checked: polled the wave (65 builds: the 62 wave builds plus ly's three resubmissions) to completion at 16:14Z - 62 succeeded, 3 failed, 0 running/pending, and every package's newest build is `succeeded`. The only failures were ly on a Codeberg 429 (logs quoted in the COPR section), answered by resubmitting the identical spec: build 11090074 succeeded 4/4 chroots; no spec edit, no Release bump, no churn (the green 11089605 NVR was already the newest published NVR throughout).

### Pattern fix (IMPROVE THE MACHINE)
- Class: update.sh asset-presence check vs GitHub pretty-printed JSON. Eight scripts grepped `"name":"<asset>"` (no space); GitHub returns `"name": "<asset>"`. The check failed every time, so designcraft/effectcraft/filmcraft/lightcraft/printcraft/vectorcraft stayed on the previous release while their linux-x86_64.rpm assets sat published (2026-10-06T12:1xZ), and the relay even recorded "assets unpublished" as fact. Fix in eb53f7a makes the grep accept optional whitespace. Third-run protection: photocraft + opencad-studio (already current) still report "already at", so the fix does not over-bump; bash -n 62/62.

### Umbriel fix narrative (the one real failure this run)
- update.sh sweep moved umbriel-git 38555de -> 9091e3f (genuine: `gh api compare` shows ahead_by=1, commit "fix(output): populate HDR metadata from display EDID").
- Upstream-intelligence read of the compare diff: meson.build adds required `dependency('libdisplay-info', version: '>=0.3.0')` wired into umbriel_core_deps; tests/meson.build adds only a unit test on umbriel_core_dep (no new external dep). No other new deps.
- Added `BuildRequires: pkgconfig(libdisplay-info) >= 0.3.0` (provider verified: libdisplay-info-devel 0.3.0 in F44; soname mapping proven, not guessed). F44 teardown PASS, pushed as d291a0c.
- COPR build 11086129: f44/f45/rawhide succeeded, f43 FAILED with `Failed to resolve the transaction: No match for argument: pkgconfig(libdisplay-info) >= 0.3.0` (builder-live.log.gz). Root cause: F43 ships libdisplay-info 0.2.0 only (proven via fedora:43 container repoquery).
- Fix: F43's 0.2.0 header already carries the used symbol `di_info_get_hdr_static_metadata` (proven by grep in F43 container), so the floor is conservative. Conditional BR (floor on F44+, unversioned on F43) + F43-only %prep sed relaxing the meson floor, both commented with F43-EOL (2026-12-09) removal note. Release 1 -> 2 + changelog entry (RELEASE REVISION DISCIPLINE b+c). Proven by clean F43 container rebuild (BUILDEP 0 / RPMBUILD 0, -2 NVR), pushed as f651046.
- Closure: COPR build 11086429 (umbriel -2) succeeded on ALL 4 chroots (f43/f44/f45/rawhide verified per-chroot); results.json shows version 0.1.0^20261006182921git9091e3f release 2.fc43.
- upstream-delta: umbriel-git 38555de->9091e3f | deps+: libdisplay-info>=0.3.0 (F43: 0.2.0 compat) | artifacts+: none | health: ok | notes: HDR EDID fix, 1 commit

### Fixes shipped this run
- umbriel-git 38555de -> 9091e3f + libdisplay-info BR (commit d291a0c): update.sh sync; compare proved 1-commit HDR delta with a new required meson dep; local f44 teardown PASS; push -> COPR 11086129 (f44/f45/rawhide green, f43 failed).
- umbriel-git -2 F43 compat (commit f651046): conditional BR + F43-only meson floor relaxation with EOL removal note; local F43 rebuild PASS; push -> COPR 11086429 succeeded on ALL 4 chroots. Priority-1 loop closed with evidence.
- mixtapes 285ba7d -> e95c9e9 + four pkgconfig version floors (commit 856d333): update.sh sync, Cargo.toml crate-feature re-read, clean f44 teardown of the new NVR PASS; push -> COPR 11086874 succeeded on ALL 4 chroots at 21:59Z (webhook proof: build appeared within seconds of the push).

### Adversarial audit (this run)
- Attack 1: the sweep's rc=0 rows prove nothing changed, so umbriel could be churn. Refuted: `git diff` showed a new upstream commit SHA; `gh api compare` confirmed ahead_by=1 with a real code delta; the meson.build dep diff is quoted above. SURVIVED.
- Attack 2: the added BR could be wrong-owner (like the helium libvulkan hijack). Refuted: provider proven via `dnf repoquery --whatprovides pkgconfig(libdisplay-info)` in-container (libdisplay-info-devel), and the demanding symbol is upstream's own meson `dependency()` call, not a guessed soname. SURVIVED.
- Attack 3: the F43 %prep sed could mask a real incompatibility (0.2.0 lacking the API). Refuted: grepped F43's installed header for `di_info_get_hdr_static_metadata` (present), and the full F43 rpmbuild compiled and linked rc 0. SURVIVED.
- Attack 4: the newest-build scan at run start claimed all-green, hiding the umbriel failure. Refuted: the scan ran before my push; the failure belonged to a build created BY this run and was caught by polling the pushed package's build list, then closed by a newer green build. SURVIVED.
- Attack 5 (fresh checker): the mixtapes floors could be guessed rather than derived. Refuted: the four version numbers are read from the extracted Source0 Cargo.toml features (`v4_18`, `v1_8`, `v1_24`, `v2_44`) and the compare patch shows `v1_7 -> v1_8` as the only dep change; no new dependency was invented. SURVIVED.
- Attack 6 (fresh checker): `LDD_NOTFOUND_COUNT=1` in the teardown log looked like a missing runtime library, which would break the app for users. Refuted: the raw log shows `grep -c 'not found'` printing `0` and the `1` is that grep's exit status; an independent second container run shows a full ldd with no `not found` line. SURVIVED.
- Attack 7 (fresh checker): pushing before the local rebuild finished could ship a package that does not compile. Refuted: builddep, rpmspec -P, sha256 and rpmlint all passed pre-push, the local rpmbuild/install/smoke then passed with the same NVR, and COPR 11086874 went green on all 4 chroots (external authority). SURVIVED.

- f43/f45/rawhide live-repo install tests not run (f44 primary for all teardowns + extra F43 umbriel rebuild; COPR 11086429 green on all 4 chroots proves compile everywhere, not install).

Observation recorded for the next run (health flag, no action taken): Fedora Rawhide itself
now ships `ly 1.5.0~rc1-1.fc46`, so a plain `dnf install ly` on rawhide resolves to the
Fedora package instead of ours. Our `ly-1.4.1-4.fc46` still installs and passes rpm -V +
`ly --version` when pinned by NVR (row above), and Fedora 43/44/45 have no official ly, so
the package stays. Same early-warning class as the retired niri/noctalia/quickshell stubs:
watch whether F44/F45 gain an official ly before considering retirement.

#### Adversarial audit (this execution)
- Attack 1: "nothing changed" could be an artifact of updaters that exit 0 without comparing.
  Refuted: the sweep prints a per-package verdict line, quoted verbatim in the 62 update.sh
  rows above ("Package is already at the latest version (0.20.2). No update needed.", ly's
  five-line HOLD with the missing vendor hashes), and the eight updater-less packages were
  checked against PyPI/crates.io/GitHub release APIs directly in this pass. SURVIVED.
- Attack 2: a live-repo install test can pass while installing nothing. Refuted: every row
  carries INSTALL_RC=0 plus `rpm -q` printing the exact NVR and `rpm -V` returning OK; the
  f44/f45 logs each show ten distinct NVRs installed. SURVIVED.
- Attack 3: "BuildRequires all resolve" could hide real misses behind the COPR. Refuted and
  reversed: the five initial misses resolved only WITH ackerman/nexus enabled and are this
  repo's own -devel packages, proved per package by `dnf repoquery --whatprovides`; no
  Fedora-official dependency is missing. SURVIVED.
- Attack 4: heroic smoke returned 1 in all four containers, which read like a broken package.
  Refuted: my smoke searched `-path '*heroic*'` case-sensitively while the package installs
  `/opt/Heroic`; the focused re-run in fedora:45 found `/opt/Heroic/resources/app.asar`
  (31766234 bytes) and `chrome-sandbox` at mode 4755 root:root, satisfying the Electron rule.
  SURVIVED.
- Attack 5: reusing this run's earlier teardown tokens would be the same "trust the previous
  pass" mistake the prompt warns about. Refuted for the three re-run packages (mangowm,
  wlroots rebuilt from the current spec in a fresh container in this execution) and admitted
  as a NAMED GAP for the seven mains not rebuilt here: their tokens come from the earlier
  execution of this same run, and their live-repo install + smoke was independently re-run
  today on f44 and f45 (table above). SURVIVED AS A STATED GAP.

Machine fix recorded so the next run cannot repeat it: both yt-dlp plugin packages install
into the `yt_dlp_plugins/extractor/` namespace, not as top-level modules, so a smoke of the
form `import yt_dlp_get_pot` is a wrong check, not a broken package. The corrected import
path above is the check to use.

### Adversarial audit (this session)

- Attack 1: update.sh rc=0 sweep means nothing changed, so the photon-studio edit could be churn. Refuted: git status after the sweep showed exactly one modified spec; prior git log held 0.1.42 only; the artifact-internal version (X-AppImage-Version=0.1.43) and changed sha256 prove a genuine upstream re-release. SURVIVED.
- Attack 2: localsend SMOKE_EXIT=1 means the package is broken. Refuted: rpmbuild+install+rpm -V all PASS; the smoke used the wrong binary name (/usr/bin/localsend vs the real /usr/bin/localsend_app from %files). Re-ran teardown with the correct name: full PASS. The bug was in my smoke, not the package. SURVIVED.
- Attack 3: upstream ProtonPlus main-branch meson says 0.6.9, so our v0.6.8 spec is stale. Refuted: release tags list v0.6.8 as newest (v0.6.8/v0.6.7/v0.6.6); 0.6.9 exists only as unreleased main-branch dev version. Release tags are the authority, not main-branch project version. SURVIVED.
- Attack 4: COPR newest-build scan covered only 37 packages in the first window, hiding failures. Refuted: re-ran with correct meta-offset pagination (1158 builds) -> all 70 spec dirs covered, COUNT_BAD=0. SURVIVED.

### Adversarial audit (this session)

- Attack 1: "the relay already said complete, so nothing changed upstream." Refuted: the session update.sh sweep (55/55 rc=0) modified exactly two specs (opencode-desktop, rootapp) - both genuinely behind upstream (stable URL redirect to 2.0.24, CDN AppImage hash changed to 0.9.146's value). Artifact-internal versions confirmed the moves (rpm -qp, X-AppImage-Version). SURVIVED.
- Attack 2: "astal-gjs/astal-gtk4 SMOKE_EXIT=2 means those packages are broken." Refuted: both are library-only packages (no binaries - %files ships only data/typelib/lib). The smoke command I used expected a binary. Re-verified via live-repo install: GJS_DATA_OK, GTK4_LIB_OK, TYPELIB_OK all present. INSTALL_EXIT=0 and RPMV_OK in the source teardown. SURVIVED.
- Attack 3: "opencode-desktop rpmlint shows no-%build-section - the spec is incomplete." Refuted: opencode-desktop is a prebuilt rpm repack - there is no %build step (the upstream rpm IS the binary). rpmlint W is a known justified warning for prebuilt packages. 0 errors. SURVIVED.
- Attack 4: "COPR newest-build scan only covered 58 packages in the first 200-build window - 5 might be hiding failures." Refuted: paginated the full build list (1148 builds, all offsets) -> 63 packages covered, COUNT_BAD=0. SURVIVED.
